## 10.5.1 Evaluation assumptions and performance evaluation

10 Release 20 NR › 10.5 Study on Integrated Sensing And Communication (ISAC) for NR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | Horizontal/vertical positioning accuracy are agreed as performance metrics for NR ISAC.<br>  - It is defined as the absolute value of the difference between the estimated horizontal/vertical position and the corresponding true position of a sensing target.<br>    - Note: in RAN1 evaluations, there should be only one estimated horizontal/vertical position corresponding to the true position of a sensing target | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506477](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506477.zip) |
| Agreement | The following evaluation parameters are agreed for the evaluation on NR ISAC.<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \|<br>\| Scenario \| UMa-AV, Optional RMa-AV \|<br>\| Carrier frequency \| Mandatory: one value either 4 GHz or 4.9GHz. optional for FR1: 6 GHz [optional for FR2: 28 GHz] \|<br>\| System bandwidth \| 100 MHz \|<br>\| Numerology \| SCS = 30kHz \|<br>\| BS Layout \| Hexagonal grid, 7 macro sites, 3 sectors per site. See Note1 3 sectors with 30, 150, 270 degrees \|<br>\| Inter-BS (2D) distance \| Uma-AV: 500m, optional 1000m, RMa-AV: 1732m \|<br>\| BS antenna height \| 25m for UMa-AV, 35m for RMa-AV \|<br>\| Wrap-round \| No wrap-round \|<br>Note1: target(s) are dropped only in the center site, and inter-BS interference is not modelled. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506478](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506478.zip) |
| Agreement | The following evaluation parameters are agreed for the evaluation on NR ISAC.<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \|<br>\| Target type \| UAV with small size (0.3m x 0.4m x 0.2m) \|<br>\| Target distribution when target(s) are dropped \| N = 5 targets per sector in the center site. Optional: N is uniformly distributed from 1 to 10. Horizontal plane: uniformly distributed in a sector Vertical plane: Uniformly distributed between 25m and 300m, optionally distributed between 1.5m and 300m \|<br>\| Mobility \| horizontal speed: uniformly distributed between 0 and 180km/h vertical speed: 0km/h \|<br>Note: N=0 will be discussed in a later proposal in relation to false alarm. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506478](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506478.zip) |
| Agreement | The following evaluation parameters are agreed for the evaluation on NR ISAC.<br>\| Parameters \| Assumptions \|<br>\| Minimum BS-target 3D distance \| 10 m \|<br>\| Minimum target-target (3D) distance \| 10 m \|<br>\| Target outdoor/indoor proportion \| 100% outdoor \|<br>\| LOS/NLOS \| LOS and NLOS \|<br>\| Orientation \| Random in horizontal domain \|<br>\| RCS model \| RCS model 1 for UAV with small size \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506478](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506478.zip) |
| Agreement | For the purpose of performance metric calculation, association of the detected object(s) and the true target(s) should fulfil at least the following conditions:<br>  - One true target is associated with at most one detected object<br>  - One detected object is associated with at most one true target<br>  - The same association applies to miss detection, false alarm probability Type 2 (if defined) and positioning/velocity accuracy<br>  - Companies to report the method used for association of the detected object(s) and the true target(s) | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506479](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506479.zip) |
| Agreement | - Missed detection probability is agreed as performance metric for NR ISAC.<br>  - It is defined as the conditional probability of not detecting the presence of a target when the target is actually present in the simulation area.<br>Where,<br>      - is the number of missed targets in the drop n, i.e., the true target not associated with any detected object<br>      - is the number of true targets in the drop n.<br>      - is total number of drops with at least one target per drop | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506479](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506479.zip) |
| Agreement | - False alarm probability is agreed as performance metric for NR ISAC.<br>  - For cases without true target dropped in simulation area, False alarm probability Type 1 is computed and reported<br>  - For cases with true targets dropped in simulation area, False alarm probability Type 2 is computed and reported<br>  - Note: both False alarm probability Types are mandatory<br>  - KPI values for False alarm probability Type 1 and 2 can be discussed separately.<br>Definitions:<br>  - False alarm probability Type 1 (no target dropped in simulation area): An object is detected when there is no target present in simulation area is considered a false alarm.<br>Where,<br>    - equal to 1 if at least one object is detected when there is no target dropped in the simulation area in the drop n, otherwise  equal to 0.<br>    - is the total number of drops without targets in the simulation area.<br>  - False alarm probability Type 2 (targets dropped in simulation area): An object is detected but not associated with any true targets in the simulation area is considered as a false alarm.<br>Where,<br>      - is the number of detected objects but not associated with any true targets in the drop n.<br>      - is the total number of detected objects in the drop n.<br>      - FFS:<br>        - Option 1:  is number of drops (N)<br>        - Option 2:  is number of drops with at least one detected object<br>Note: the number of targets should be reported by companies when providing False alarm probability Type 2 | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506479](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506479.zip) |
| Agreement | - Velocity accuracy is agreed as performance metric for NR ISAC.<br>  - Velocity accuracy is defined as the absolute value of the difference between the estimated velocity and the corresponding true velocity of a sensing target.<br>  - For single TRP monostatic sensing,<br>    - The radial velocity accuracy can be estimated<br>      - The true radial velocity is the projection of true velocity on the direction from TRP to target for TRP monostatic.<br>    - The true velocity accuracy can be estimated. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506479](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506479.zip) |

| Agreement | For PRACH performance evaluation for existing PRACH formats, adopt the following methods:<br>- Baseline: Analytical characterization of performance based on e.g. properties of ZC sequences and values of differential Delay / Doppler.<br>- Optional: Link level evaluations<br>  - The following table is proposed for LLS parameters<br>\| Configurations \| S/L-band \| Ka/Ku-band \|<br>\| Carrier Frequency \| 2 GHz \| For Ka: UL 30GHz, for Ku: UL 14 GHz \|<br>\| Channel Model \| Baseline NTN TDL-C rural model in TR38.811 \| Baseline NTN TDL-C rural model in TR38.811 \|<br>\| Antenna Configuration at the TRP (satellite) \| 1 Rx \| 1 Rx \|<br>\| Antenna Configuration at the UE \| A single omni-directional antenna element \| VSAT with 60 cm equivalent aperture diameter \|<br>\| PRACH format \| To be reported by companies. \| To be reported by companies \|<br>\| PRACH configuration \| To be reported by companies. \| To be reported by companies. \|<br>\| Metric \| PRACH detection rate, FAR (Based on the preamble pool size is not less than 64), CDF of estimation error for frequency/timing \| PRACH detection rate, FAR (Based on the preamble pool size is not less than 64), CDF of estimation error for frequency/timing \|<br>\| Receiver \| Companies to report the receiver for PRACH detection. \| Companies to report the receiver for PRACH detection. \| | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506612](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506612.zip) |
| Agreement | For evaluation regarding GNSS-resilient operation, based on GNSS information or the lack of it, it is assumed that the UE has a horizontal location uncertainty within an area<br>- Case a: the location uncertainty area is the area served by the cell or beam.<br>- Case b: the location uncertainty area is a circle of radius X km<br>  - X to be reported<br>UE altitude error should be taken into account.<br>- UE altitude error to be reported.<br>For both Case a and Case b above, RAN1 to evaluate at least:<br>- Differential delay values<br>- Differential frequency offset/Doppler values | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>[R1-2506612](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Docs/R1-2506612.zip) |

| Agreement | - Identify the high-level aspects which impact on the 6GR sync signal structure and associated periodicity. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - Identify the high-level aspects which impact on the NR-6GR MRSS support<br>  - Including the lessons learned from LTE-NR DSS | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - Study and identify the lessons learned from NR BWP framework | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - Study and identify the lessons learned from NR spectrum utilization and aggregation framework<br>  - DC is subject to RANP decision in June 2026<br>  - Note: MRSS aspects are separate discussion | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - Study the following smallest maximum supported RF and BB UE BW without spectrum aggregation for at least one low-tier device type supported by 6GR framework from physical layer perspective, subject to further discussion and confirmation in RAN<br>  - Opt1: 3MHz<br>  - Opt2: 5MHz<br>  - Opt3: 10MHz<br>  - Opt4: 20MHz<br>  - FFS: the UL bandwidth may be different to the DL bandwidth<br>  - FFS: the bandwidth value may be different for different SCS, duplex modes, and bands.<br>  - FFS: whether RF and BB UE BW are same or different | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - Study and identify the lessons learned from NR duplex modes<br>- On 6GR duplexing study, RAN1 considers at least following duplex types<br>  - FD-FDD<br>  - Semi-static TDD<br>  - gNB semi-static SBFD<br>  - HD-FDD on UE side<br>  - Dynamic TDD<br>- Study whether to consider following duplexing types<br>  - gNB dynamic SBFD<br>  - UE SBFD<br>  - gNB FD<br>  - Note: Other duplex modes are not precluded | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |
| Agreement | - For harmonized 6GR design for TN and NTN, RAN1 studies to identify the technical aspects affected by NTN characteristics, as well as lessons learned from NR/IoT NTN | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Overall]` |

## 11.2 Evaluation assumptions for 6GR air interface

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |

| Agreement | - The deployment scenarios in TR38.914 should be considered for evaluation assumption<br>- The common evaluation assumptions including the antenna modelling, general system-level simulation assumptions (including the carrier frequency, bandwidth and subcarrier spacing used for link-level simulation) for the deployment scenarios in TR38.914, link budget and traffic models will be discussed in AI 11.2<br>  - Other assumptions including for link-level simulation specific to each technical topic will be separately discussed under each individual agenda.<br>  - Note: Subcarrier spacing decision is up to AI 11.3.2. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| Conclusion | - Template in R1-2506582 is to be used for collecting inputs from companies.<br>  - Additional NTN or TN assumptions, if any, or any necessary change of the parameters, are to be incorporated into the updated one of R1-2506582. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |
| Agreement | - Study which of the following traffic models are to be used for 6G evaluations, e.g.,<br>  - Full buffer<br>  - FTP Model 1 (in TR 36.814)<br>  - FTP Model 2 (in TR 36.814)<br>  - FTP Model 3 (in TR 36.872)<br>  - XR Traffic models (in TR 38.838)<br>  - VoIP model (as in TR 36.814)<br>  - Instant message (as in TR 38.840)<br>- Study whether to introduce the following traffic models for 6G evaluations considering, e.g.,<br>  - FTP-3 variant with packet delay budget requirement<br>    - Details FFS<br>  - New traffic model considering a mixed/variable packet size and the associated time domain behaviors (e.g., time between adjacent packet arrivals, packet delay budget)<br>    - Details FFS<br>  - New traffic model(s) considering the new use cases or services, e.g., AI/ML services, immersive communication services, etc.<br>    - Details FFS<br>- Study whether to introduce new/additional approaches that can reflect the impact of bidirectional traffic flows on performance metrics (e.g., impact of UL TCP ACK latency on DL throughput/latency)<br>Note: Whether/how to consider the combination of traffic model and loading level will be studied under individual agendas. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Evaluation]` |

## 11.3.1 Waveform

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.3 Waveform and frame structure for 6GR air interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | CP-OFDM and DFT-s-OFDM waveforms as defined in 5G NR are supported as the basis for 6GR for uplink<br>- Enhancements/modifications on CP-OFDM/DFT-s-OFDM will be studied as potential additions<br>- Other OFDM based waveforms are not precluded. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |
| Agreement | CP-OFDM waveform as defined in 5G NR is supported as the basis for 6GR for downlink<br>- Enhancements/modifications on CP-OFDM will be studied as potential additions<br>- DFT-s-OFDM or any other OFDM-based waveform will be studied as a potential additional waveform for downlink<br>Note: proponents to identify at least the target use cases, signals/channels to use the waveform, and how the proposal is intended (if applicable) to support multiplexing with CP-OFDM, including MRSS, and how multi-user multiplexing is supported, etc.<br>Note:<br>Proponents are encouraged to provide more detailed information on their proposals for the next meeting, e.g.:<br>Proponents to characterize the main motivation for modification/additional waveform proposals:<br>- Targeted link direction, i.e. DL, UL or both<br>- Targeted use case (e.g. NTN, specific frequency range, etc.), if any<br>- Potential motivations metrics used, and quantified gains for a proposal, e.g.<br>  - Coverage<br>  - Network energy efficiency<br>  - UE energy efficiency<br>  - Spectral efficiency<br>  - High speed tolerance<br>  - Scheduling flexibility<br>  - Integration with ISAC<br>Proponents provide information on the following aspects, if applicable<br>- MRSS compatibility<br>- Target channels/signals, e.g. all channels, PxSCH only, etc.<br>- MIMO (SU and MU-MIMO) compatibility<br>- Target modulations, and impact to other modulations, if applicable<br>- Multi-user multiplexing/scheduling flexibility<br>- Multiplexing/coexistence with baseline waveforms<br>- Impact on synchronization and initial access<br>- Expected specification impact<br>- Transmitter/receiver complexity and impact to power consumption. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Waveform]` |

## 11.3.2 Frame structure

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.3 Waveform and frame structure for 6GR air interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - 6GR takes the following SCS as start point for discussion for all the signals/channels except PRACH.<br>  - For sub 6GHz<br>    - The following subcarrier spacing is at least supported<br>      - 15kHz SCS for FDD, 30kHz SCS for TDD<br>    - FFS: 30kHz SCS for FDD for around e.g., 1-2.5GHz<br>    - FFS: 7.5kHz SCS for sub1GHz (FDD)<br>    - Whether to discuss the FFS will be subject to RANP decision.<br>  - For around 7GHz<br>    - The following subcarrier spacing options can be studied<br>      - 30kHz, 60kHz<br>  - FFS: For around 15GHz<br>    - The following subcarrier spacing options can be studied<br>      - 30kHz, 60kHz, 120kHz<br>    - Whether to discuss it will be subject to RANP decision<br>  - For between 24.25GHz - 52.6GHz<br>    - Subcarrier spacing 120kHz is supported<br>  - FFS whether to allow using additional subcarrier spacing for SSB<br>- FFS subcarrier spacing for PRACH and up to initial access discussion. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| Conclusion | - Numerologies for sensing is up to sensing agenda discussion. | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |
| Agreement | - 6GR supports normal cyclic prefix, i.e., same as the normal CP defined in NR.<br>  - FFS potential need for other CP | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-Frame structure]` |

## 11.4.2 Modulation, joint channel coding and modulation

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR › 11.4 Channel coding and modulation for 6GR interface

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | - For 6GR DL, 5G NR uniform QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, 256QAM and 1024QAM are supported as basis for study for data channel<br>  - FFS: Enhancements and other modulation schemes<br>- For 6GR UL, 5G NR uniform QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, and 256QAM are supported as basis for study for CP-OFDM for data channel<br>  - FFS: Enhancements and other modulation schemes<br>- For 6GR UL, 5G NR pi/2 BPSK, uniform QPSK, 16QAM, 64QAM, and 256QAM are supported as basis for study for DFT-s-OFDM for data channel<br>  - FFS: Enhancements and other modulation schemes | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip) |

## 11.6 AI/ML in 6GR interface

11 Rel-20 Study of 6GR

| 구분 | 내용 | 출처 |
|---|---|---|
| Agreement | For 6GR AI/ML use cases identification/categorization, for each (sub-)use case proposed, proponent companies are encouraged to study and report the following:<br>- Definition of each (sub-)use case, including at least AI/ML model input/output<br>- The evaluation assumption, methodology, KPIs, benchmark, and preliminary simulation results<br>- Assumption on training types, e.g.,<br>  - offline training, online training/finetuning<br>  - Label construction (if applicable), including whether/how to obtain label data for model training<br>- Assumption on model location for inference, e.g., UE-sided model, NW-sided model, and two-sided model<br>- Collaboration/interaction between UE and NW, e.g.,<br>  - no collaboration/interaction<br>  - UE/Network collaboration targeting at separate or joint ML operation<br>- High level potential specification impact | [Chair notes RAN1#122 eom](https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/tsg_ran/WG1_RL1/TSGR1_122/Inbox/Chair_notes/Chair%20notes%20RAN1%23122%20eom.zip)<br>`[122-R20-6GR-AI/ML]` |
